Negotiation then tests how the directive will operate between speakers.","ayah_refs":["91:3","91:11","91:14","91:13","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Sequence, Remainder, and Consequence","parent_semantic_invariant":"What comes after may follow, replace, remain as a trace, reverse course, or arrive as the consequence of an act.","parent_surprising_reach":"Temporal order is embodied in heels and tails, then extended into residue, retreat, punishment, and repeated action.","key":"A","name":"Following, Procession, and Iteration","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Participants move one after another, replace predecessors, or repeat a prior action.","active_motifs":"direct following `ت ل و:B001/m01`; successive groups `ر س ل:B005/m01`; succession and replacement `ع ق ب:B005/m01`; repeated return to an action `ع ق ب:B009/m01`; followers behind leaders `ذ ن ب:B003/m01`; rapid collective setting-out `ب ع ث:B004/m01`; immediate action without delay `ك ذ ب:B005/m01`","ayah_anchors":"91:2 `تلاها`; 91:11 `كذبت`; 91:12 `انبعث`; 91:13 `رسول`; 91:14 `بذنبهم` and `كذبوه`; 91:15 `عقباها`","synthesis":"Sequence can be linear, substitutive, or iterative. A follower occupies the wake of another, successive groups preserve the line, and repetition turns one passage into a recurring cycle.","ayah_refs":["91:2","91:11","91:12","91:13","91:14","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Sequence, Remainder, and Consequence","parent_semantic_invariant":"What comes after may follow, replace, remain as a trace, reverse course, or arrive as the consequence of an act.","parent_surprising_reach":"Temporal order is embodied in heels and tails, then extended into residue, retreat, punishment, and repeated action.","key":"B","name":"Heel, Tail, Trace, and Remainder","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"The rear of a moving body becomes the place where traces and remnants persist.","active_motifs":"heel and footprint `ع ق ب:B002/m01`; tail or terminal rear `ذ ن ب:B002/m02`; remaining portion `ت ل و:B003/m01`; residue and aftereffect `ع ق ب:B011/m01`; last or final part `ع ق ب:B006/m02`","ayah_anchors":"91:2 `تلاها`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:14 `بذنبهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`","synthesis":"Rear anatomy supplies a concrete model of aftermath. Heel and tail mark the path behind a body, while residue and remainder are what an event leaves in the same trailing position.","ayah_refs":["91:2","91:8","91:14","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Sequence, Remainder, and Consequence","parent_semantic_invariant":"What comes after may follow, replace, remain as a trace, reverse course, or arrive as the consequence of an act.","parent_surprising_reach":"Temporal order is embodied in heels and tails, then extended into residue, retreat, punishment, and repeated action.","key":"C","name":"Retreat, Desertion, and Interrupted Motion","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Forward movement is abandoned, reversed, or broken by a sudden halt.","active_motifs":"abandonment after companionship `ت ل و:B005/m01`; turning back on the heel `ع ق ب:B003/m01`; failed charge or cowardly stop `ك ذ ب:B004/m01`; animal running then halting `ك ذ ب:B007/m01`; withholding after apparent availability `ش م س:B006/m01`","ayah_anchors":"91:1 `الشمس`; 91:2 `تلاها`; 91:11 and 91:14 forms of `كذب`; 91:15 `عقباها`","synthesis":"Each motif begins with an expected continuation and then violates it. Companion, attacker, animal, or provider withdraws from the established line, making reversal a social as well as kinetic break.","ayah_refs":["91:1","91:2","91:11","91:14","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Sequence, Remainder, and Consequence","parent_semantic_invariant":"What comes after may follow, replace, remain as a trace, reverse course, or arrive as the consequence of an act.","parent_surprising_reach":"Temporal order is embodied in heels and tails, then extended into residue, retreat, punishment, and repeated action.","key":"D","name":"Sin, Outcome, and Punishment","reading_type":"surface-primary","scene_or_process":"An offense is followed by its terminal result and punitive repayment.","active_motifs":"sin or culpable act `ذ ن ب:B001/m01`; allotted share of punishment `ذ ن ب:B006/m01`; consequence and final outcome `ع ق ب:B006/m01`; punishment after wrongdoing `ع ق ب:B007/m01`; annihilating retribution `د م د م:B001/m01`; fear of an expected harm `خ و ف:B001/m01`","ayah_anchors":"91:14 `دمدم`, `بذنبهم`, and `فسواها`; 91:15 `يخاف عقباها`","synthesis":"Wrongdoing initiates a causal sequence whose last member is not arbitrary: consequence follows the offense as its allotted share. Punishment closes the sequence through total leveling, while fear anticipates that terminal outcome.","ayah_refs":["91:14","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Transgression, Contest, and Collective Force","parent_semantic_invariant":"Force crosses a limit, confronts an opponent, competes for dominance, gathers collectively, and may culminate in destructive overpowering.","parent_surprising_reach":"Moral rebellion shares a shape with hydraulic overflow, animal restiveness, boasting contests, crowd pressure, and sudden mass arrival.","key":"C","name":"Competition and Overcoming","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Rivals test endurance, fear, prestige, or skill until one overcomes the other.","active_motifs":"contest in fear `خ و ف:B003/m01`; rivalry and boasting `س م و:B007/m01`; deceptive victory in play `ق م ر:B007/m02`; negotiated contest `ق و ل:B009/m01`","ayah_anchors":"91:2 `القمر`; 91:5 `السماء`; 91:12 `أشقاها`; 91:13 `قال`; 91:15 `يخاف`","synthesis":"Competition measures relative capacity rather than absolute strength. Fear, prestige, strategy, and speech become different arenas in which one participant seeks to exceed another.","ayah_refs":["91:2","91:5","91:12","91:13","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Animal Movement and Wild Ecology","parent_semantic_invariant":"Animals are roused, ridden, restrained, injured, herded, hunted, fed, or located within a habitat structured by water and vegetation.","parent_surprising_reach":"The she-camel scene opens into riding technology, restive temperament, moonlit hunting, wetland birds, herd succession, and nocturnal feeding routines.","key":"B","name":"Restiveness, Hoof Protection, and Immobilization","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A skittish animal resists mounting, guards an injured hoof, or becomes unable to move.","active_motifs":"restive animal `ش م س:B002/m01`; immobilizing saddle injury or shock `ع ق ر:B003/m01`; guarded hoof and slight lameness `و ق ي:B003/m01`; tail and hindpart `ذ ن ب:B002/m01`; heel tendon `ع ق ب:B001/m01`; non-wounding saddle `و ق ي:B003/m02`","ayah_anchors":"91:1 `الشمس`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:14 `بذنبهم` and `عقروها`; 91:15 `عقباها`","synthesis":"Restiveness and pain are read through altered movement. The animal withholds its back, protects the hoof, or loses locomotion entirely; careful saddle design provides the counter-mechanism that prevents injury.","ayah_refs":["91:1","91:8","91:14","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Animal Movement and Wild Ecology","parent_semantic_invariant":"Animals are roused, ridden, restrained, injured, herded, hunted, fed, or located within a habitat structured by water and vegetation.","parent_surprising_reach":"The she-camel scene opens into riding technology, restive temperament, moonlit hunting, wetland birds, herd succession, and nocturnal feeding routines.","key":"C","name":"Hamstringing and Bodily Wound","reading_type":"surface-primary","scene_or_process":"A leg or tendon is cut, the animal falls, and slaughter or lasting injury follows.","active_motifs":"cutting the legs of a mount `ع ق ر:B002/m01`; bodily wound `ع ق ر:B001/m01`; heel tendon `ع ق ب:B001/m01`; animal hindpart `ذ ن ب:B002/m01`; she-camel `ن و ق:B002/m01`","ayah_anchors":"91:13 `ناقة`; 91:14 `عقروها` and `بذنبهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`","synthesis":"The act targets the animal’s locomotive structure. Hindpart, heel tendon, and cut legs form a precise injury scene in which disabling movement becomes the immediate path to the animal’s death.","ayah_refs":["91:13","91:14","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Animal Movement and Wild Ecology","parent_semantic_invariant":"Animals are roused, ridden, restrained, injured, herded, hunted, fed, or located within a habitat structured by water and vegetation.","parent_surprising_reach":"The she-camel scene opens into riding technology, restive temperament, moonlit hunting, wetland birds, herd succession, and nocturnal feeding routines.","key":"E","name":"Raptors, Carrion, and Wetland Birds","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Birds occupy distinct ecological roles around carrion, prey, reeds, and water.","active_motifs":"eagle or raptor `ع ق ب:B013/m01`; male partridge `ع ق ب:B014/m02`; vultures circling a kill `ط ح و:B003/m01`; shrike `و ق ي:B005/m01`; turtledove `ق م ر:B012/m01`; water-fed papyrus or cane `س ق ي:B008/m01`; abundant water `ر ب ب:B013/m01`","ayah_anchors":"91:2 `القمر`; 91:6 `طحاها`; 91:8 `تقواها`; 91:13 `سقياها`; 91:14 `ربهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`","synthesis":"The bird class resolves into habitats and feeding roles rather than a bare taxonomy. Raptors and vultures occupy pursuit and carrion scenes, while shrike and turtledove connect to water-fed cane and the margins of a wet habitat.","ayah_refs":["91:2","91:6","91:8","91:13","91:14","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Kinship, Development, and Marital Exchange","parent_semantic_invariant":"Persons enter households through birth, nurture, descent, courtship, marriage, or compensatory exchange.","parent_surprising_reach":"Genealogical following extends to fosterage and infertility, while unveiling and gifts turn visibility into a structured marital transition.","key":"A","name":"Birth, Fosterage, and Descent","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A child is born, follows the mother, is nurtured by a household, and continues a line of descent.","active_motifs":"birth and newborn `ن ف س:B005/m01`; young following its mother `ت ل و:B006/m01`; sonship and descent `ب ن ي:B007/m01`; small derivative offshoot `ب ن ي:B008/m01`; fostered child `ر ب ب:B005/m01`; caregiver or nurse `ر ب ب:B005/m02`; descendants `ع ق ب:B004/m01`; newborn or lactating animal `ر ب ب:B009/m01`","ayah_anchors":"91:2 `تلاها`; 91:5 `بناها`; 91:7 `نفس`; 91:14 `ربهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`","synthesis":"Birth begins a sequence of dependence and continuation. The young follows the mother, nurture incorporates it into a household, and descent carries that household forward beyond the present generation.","ayah_refs":["91:2","91:5","91:7","91:14","91:15"]},{"parent_channel":"Authority, Worship, and Covenant","parent_semantic_invariant":"Legitimate or illegitimate authority establishes worship, differentiates ranks, binds parties, and enforces judgment.","parent_surprising_reach":"Sacred authority extends lexically into scholars, local rulers, captains, deacons, treaties, prayer, sacrifice, and imprecation.","key":"C","name":"Covenant, Obligation, and Judgment","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Parties enter a binding relation whose terms can be enforced, contested, or answered by consequence.","active_motifs":"covenant and pledged protection `ر ب ب:B011/m01`; guarantee or attached right `ت ل و:B004/m01`; substitute or commercial guarantee `ع ق ب:B010/m01`; objection or rebuttal `ع ق ب:B008/m01`; punishment after offense `ع ق ب:B007/m01`","ayah_anchors":"91:2 `تلاها`; 91:14 `ربهم`; 91:15 `عقباها`","synthesis":"A covenant causes an obligation to follow its holder. ## Your response

Write in tr. Produce your response as three parts, in this order. If you return one combined response, label the parts clearly. If an orchestrator asks you to write the parts into separate files, omit wrapper labels from the prose and evidence files; the file path already supplies the label.

1. **The prose** — per the output contract in `COMMENTARY_SPEC.md` §5 and the task document above: continuous, single voice, no provenance markers, no headers named after evidence layers.
2. **The evidence surface** — separate from the prose, addressable per phrase, per `PRINCIPLES.md` §12.
3. A section headed exactly `=== PROMPT FRICTION ===` reporting honestly where the specification above was unclear, self-contradictory, underspecified, or impossible to follow, and any point where you had to invent a rule to proceed. Report this section every time, even if the run went cleanly — say so explicitly if you found nothing. This is how the specification gets improved.

## V2 Rendering Profile — 5.6 Sol High

This run tests whether `5.6-sol-high` can increase lexical depth while preserving
reader-facing clarity.

Increase depth through semantic precision, not additional bulk.

Before drafting, silently build a coverage ledger:

- include every `must_integrate` topic;
- include every `candidate` with a reader payoff not already expressed;
- if `v12_reader_responses` is absent because the default workflow retired
  per-ayah focus runs, do not infer a `stage_00` isolated response or
  confidence movement;
- if `v12_focus_trace_hermetic` is present, use its `baseline_models`,
  `context_deltas`, and `surprising_valid_outliers` as reconstructed
  before/after evidence. Do not call it a staged reveal transcript. Preserve
  surprising outliers when they remain anchored in this ayah, especially
  secondary split-root activations;
- include a regular reader-walk, plus/minus-5 reader-walk, whole-surah reading,
  or cross-run-publication item only when it adds a distinct retrospective
  insight or coverage check;
- use branch inventories to support explanations; they create no standalone
  prose obligation. When fallback inventories are present, treat them as
  restricted to this ayah's roots and anchored citations, not as a whole-surah
  obligation.
- treat `root_lexicon` as full dictionary/gloss support, not branch-filtered.
  It uses QAC-to-Furuq root mapping; when a QAC root is split, non-dominant
  Furuq targets are included as additional root entries and recorded in
  coverage. If multiple QAC roots map to the same Furuq root, the shared entry
  records all source roots in `qac_roots_ar` / `qac_root_mappings`; use those
  fields for evidence attribution when present, not the legacy single `root_ar`
  alone. Dictionary/gloss branches are evidence support, not independent
  obligations, unless tied to this ayah's word, topic, reader payoff, or cited
  relation. Full field means all distinct reader payoffs from activated
  material, not every dictionary branch for the root.
- if `channel_generated_outputs` lists quran-data files and this run gives you
  file access, read only those listed files when channel-family/path detail is
  necessary. Treat them as candidate/family/path evidence, not as an adjudicated
  channel ledger. State B channel restrictions still apply.

For each critical word, preserve these distinct layers when available:

1. local grammatical work;
2. locally selected sense;
3. coherent pressure supplied by activated or cited root branches;
4. one form, sound, rarity, or variant observation with a unique payoff;
5. later contextual change, compressed to its final reader-visible result.

Do not flatten these layers into a general metaphor. Deepen generic summaries by
naming the exact lexical mechanism. Ordering grammar and local meaning first is
grounding, not truth-ranking.

At first mention of an ayah word, use a structured Arabic surface span:
`{ar:surface_form, tr:Turkish-readable transliteration, gloss:target-language meaning}`.
Use the same full span again when the prose returns to that word after moving to
another word or another paragraph. Inside one short local sequence, after a full
span has just been given, a Turkish label or transliteration is enough. Explain
every technical term in the same sentence. Keep raw roots, root skeletons,
letter-by-letter root transliterations, and branch IDs in the evidence surface.
In prose, attach root discussion to the surface word:
`{ar:ٱلْعَادِيَاتِ, tr:el-âdiyât, gloss:koşup atılanlar} kelimesinin bağlı
olduğu kök alanı...`, not `ʿ-d-v kökü...`.

A paragraph may begin with the ayah's surface, a concrete image, or a
reader-facing claim. It may not begin with a bare grammar label or stacked
abstractions. Prefer "Âyet önce hamdi Allah'a verir; sabit ad cümlesi bu hamdi
yerleşik bir hüküm olarak taşır" over "Bu âyet, tek bir isim cümlesiyle yerleşik
bir hüküm kurar."

For this short ayah, keep at least two-thirds of the prose on its own wording.
Compress all later developments into at most three paragraphs and end with one
plain synthesis paragraph.

Use `v12_cross_run_publication`, if present, only as a compact coverage/priority
check derived from regular and plus/minus-5 reader runs. Do not copy it as prose,
and do not let it override local bundle evidence.

Use an explicit negative predicate only to correct a likely misconception,
protect the primary sense from replacement, or preserve live counter-evidence.
Default ceiling for this ayah: three. Never stack two negatives in one sentence.
During final revision, rewrite all other negatives as positive predication.
Do not drive negation to zero by default: if no explicit negative is needed,
include a profile-specific style audit in friction confirming that there was no
live misconception, replacement risk, or counter-evidence requiring one.

Pass only if every paragraph has one distinct reader payoff and the prose remains
clear with the evidence surface closed.
